This confirms the analytic estimate of Proposition~\ref{prop:threshold}:
the gap decays like the quantum coherence surviving $Ln$ depolarizing hits.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Asymmetry: KI saturates, XX increases]
The most striking feature is that $h^{(\mathrm{KI})}(p)=1$ exactly for all
tested $p\in[0,0.5]$.  This is not noise-resistant scrambling but rather a
consequence of the dual-unitary measurement structure: any state, however
noisy, gives a uniform (50/50) measurement at site~$0$ after one dual-unitary
step.  The noise only affects the \emph{XX chain}, whose sub-maximal
entropy is raised by noise toward the KI baseline.  This is a sharply
non-classical effect: adding noise increases the measured entropy of the
integrable system.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Scrambling, Topological Order, and Measurement-Induced Transitions}
\label{sec:mit}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Connection to measurement-induced phase transitions}

The time-AFL entropy $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ measures the
information content of a sequence of quantum measurements on a
time-evolving system.  This is precisely the setting of
\emph{measurement-induced phase transitions} (MIPT) \cite{nag2019,skinner2019},
where the competition between unitary scrambling and quantum measurements
produces a phase transition in the entanglement entropy.

\begin{remark}[AFL as order parameter for MIPT]
In MIPT studies: the entanglement entropy of the system after $n$ random
measurements transitions between a ``volume-law'' phase (chaotic, high
measurement rate) and an ``area-law'' phase (localised, low measurement
rate).  The time-AFL entropy $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ is the
\emph{information rate} of the measurement record, which is dual to the
entanglement entropy of the unmeasured remainder.  Specifically:
\begin{itemize}
\item High $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ (KI, dual-unitary): the
  measurements provide $n\log d$ bits per $n$ steps --- volume law information.
\item Low $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ (XX, integrable): measurements
  provide less than $n\log d$ bits --- area-law-like information.
\end{itemize}
The AFL chaos indicator is therefore the \emph{mutual information} perspective
on the MIPT order parameter.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Topological phases and scrambling suppression}

In topological phases (e.g., the toric code, fractional quantum Hall states),
local measurements at site~$0$ cannot distinguish topological sectors because
all local observables are trivial (have zero expectation in the ground state
superposition).  This means:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} = 0
\]
for topologically ordered ground states in the zero-temperature limit
$\beta\to\infty$.  The AFL entropy therefore provides a \emph{topological
indicator}: $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} = 0$ at zero temperature
implies either integrability or topological protection, while
$h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} > 0$ implies genuine scrambling.

\begin{conjecture}[Topological Suppression of AFL]
\label{conj:topo}
For a topologically ordered ground state $|\psi_{\mathrm{top}}\rangle$
on a torus geometry of $L\times L$ qudits:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(\omega_{\psi_{\mathrm{top}}})
  = 0 \quad\text{for any local OPU on a single site.}
\]
This reflects the fact that local measurements cannot access topological
degrees of freedom, which are globally encoded.
\end{conjecture}

\subsection{Final synthesis}

The full landscape of quantum chaos as measured by the time-AFL entropy
is:
\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{array}{lll}
\hline
\text{System} & h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} & \text{Noise robustness}\\
\hline
\text{Dual-unitary (KI, }\pi/4) & \log d & \text{Local: robust; Global: robust}\\
\text{Chaotic (KI, }J\ne\pi/4) & 0 < h < \log d & \text{Decreases with noise}\\
\text{Integrable (XX)} & h < \log d & \text{Global noise raises }h\\
\text{Topological ground state} & 0 & \text{Gapped: no dynamics}\\
\text{Amplitude damping fixed pt} & 0 & \text{Trivially zero}\\
\hline
\end{array}
\]
The integrable-chaotic gap:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Persists} under local noise (amplitude damping on site~$0$)
  for any $\gamma<1$.
\item \textbf{Collapses} under global noise (depolarizing on all sites)
  at a threshold $p_{1/2}\sim(\log 2)/(Ln)$.
\item Is \textbf{absent} for topological phases (zero for both).
\end{itemize}
This completes the quantum Pesin framework: the time-AFL entropy is a
rigorous, computable, and physically meaningful indicator of quantum chaos,
with a proven upper bound $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}\leq v_B\log d$
and an exact equality at maximal chaos (dual-unitary circuits).

%=======================================================================
\section{Hybrid Quantum Circuits and AFL Entropy as an MIPT Order Parameter}
\label{sec:mipt_afl}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Hybrid circuit model}

A \emph{hybrid quantum circuit} on $L$ qudits (local dimension $d$) is
defined by alternating rounds of unitary gates and projective measurements:

\begin{definition}[Hybrid circuit]\label{def:hybrid}
A $(T,p)$-hybrid circuit on $L$ qudits consists of $T$ layers, each
comprising:
\begin{enumerate}
\item A \emph{unitary layer} $U$ (brick-wall random two-qudit gates in a
  1D geometry).
\item For each site $k=0,\ldots,L-1$: a projective measurement in the
  computational basis with probability $p\in[0,1]$.
\end{enumerate}
The parameter $p$ is the \emph{measurement rate}.
\end{definition}

The AFL entropy we compute is the \emph{site-$0$ time-AFL entropy}
$h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ defined in Section~\ref{sec:time_afl}
(Sections~35--38): the OPU uses projectors $\{P_0,P_1\}$ on site~$0$ and
the time automorphism $\Theta = \mathrm{Ad}_U$.  The hybrid circuit
introduces measurements on all sites \emph{between} the OPU steps,
converting the closed-system AFL to an \emph{open-system} AFL.

\subsection{MIPT and the information-theoretic picture}

The \emph{measurement-induced phase transition} (MIPT) \cite{skinner2019}
in 1D hybrid circuits (random Clifford or Haar-random gates) produces a
sharp transition at a critical measurement rate $p_c$ between two phases:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Volume-law phase} ($p < p_c$): the entanglement entropy of
  any subregion scales as $S_{\mathrm{ent}} \sim |A|$ (volume).  Unitary
  scrambling dominates over measurement-induced collapse.
\item \textbf{Area-law phase} ($p > p_c$): $S_{\mathrm{ent}} \sim \partial A|$
  (area, i.e.\ $O(1)$ in 1D).  Measurements purify the state faster than
  scrambling creates entanglement.
\end{itemize}

The time-AFL entropy $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ captures the
\emph{information content of the measurement record} at site~$0$:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}
  = \lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}S\!\bigl(\rho[Z^{(n)}]\bigr),
\]
where $\rho[Z^{(n)}]_{I,J} = \tfrac{1}{D}\mathrm{Tr}(Z_J^\dagger Z_I)$ and
the $Z_I$ are the OPU elements built from alternating measurement and
unitary steps.

\begin{theorem}[Volume-law AFL saturation]
\label{thm:vol_law_afl}
In the volume-law phase ($p<p_c$) of a 1D hybrid circuit with
dual-unitary scrambling layers ($J=g=\pi/4$):
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} = \log d
  \quad \text{for all } p \in [0,1).
\]
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By Theorem~\ref{thm:local_gap} (Section~\ref{sec:threshold_theory}), for
the dual-unitary kicked-Ising circuit, amplitude damping on site~$0$ at
any rate $\gamma<1$ leaves $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} = \log d$
unchanged.  Projective measurements on site~$0$ at rate $p<1$ are a
special case of amplitude damping at rate $\gamma < 1$.  Therefore the
result follows immediately from the dual-unitary mixing property: after
each unitary step the site-$0$ reduced state is exactly $I_2/2$, making
the next measurement uniformly random regardless of the rate $p$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Area-law AFL reduction]
\label{thm:area_law_afl}
For a non-scrambling (integrable) circuit $U_{\mathrm{int}}$ and
measurement rate $p>0$:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(p) \leq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(0)
  < \log d.
\]
In the large-$L$ limit: if $U_{\mathrm{int}}$ produces a nearly product
state (area-law entanglement), then
$h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(p) \to 0$ as $L\to\infty$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The first inequality follows from the data-processing inequality
(Theorem~\ref{thm:noise_suppress}): adding measurements is a quantum
channel, and quantum channels cannot increase AFL entropy.  The second
inequality ($< \log d$) was established for the XX chain in
Section~\ref{sec:ff}.

For the large-$L$ limit: an area-law circuit produces a ground state
$|\psi\rangle$ that is well-approximated by a matrix-product state (MPS)
with bond dimension $\chi = O(1)$.  The reduced density matrix on site~$0$
has at most $2\chi$ nonzero eigenvalues and a fixed spectrum independent
of $L$.  Repeated measurements project the state onto computational basis
elements, and the consecutive measurement outcomes on site~$0$ form a
classical Markov chain with a finite transition matrix.  As $L\to\infty$
with $p$ fixed, the transition matrix converges to a deterministic
permutation (area-law: site~$0$ is in a nearly definite state after each
measurement), giving $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}\to 0$.
\end{proof}

\begin{conjecture}[AFL-MIPT order parameter]
\label{conj:mipt_order}
For a 1D hybrid circuit with Haar-random two-qudit gates and measurement
rate $p$:
\[
  \lim_{L\to\infty} h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(L,p)
  = \begin{cases}
      \log d & p < p_c \\
      h^*(p) & p > p_c
    \end{cases}
\]
where $h^*(p) < \log d$ and $h^*(p)\to 0$ as $p\to 1$.
The transition at $p_c$ is sharp (non-analytic in $L\to\infty$ limit), and
$h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ is an order parameter for the MIPT.
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[MIPT vs.\ noise robustness]
Theorem~\ref{thm:vol_law_afl} shows that the volume-law phase is
\emph{dual to the noise robustness} of Section~\ref{sec:threshold_theory}:
both are manifestations of the dual-unitary mixing property.  The area-law
phase corresponds to the ``global noise collapse'' scenario of
Section~\ref{sec:threshold_theory}: frequent measurements act like global
noise, destroying quantum coherences and collapsing $h$ toward~$0$.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Numerical AFL-MIPT Phase Diagram}
\label{sec:mipt_numerics}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Mean-field model and results}

The exact trajectory average for the AFL density matrix in a hybrid
circuit is computationally expensive (requires $O(k^{2n} D^2)$ memory).
We use a \emph{mean-field approximation}: the effective measurement
suppression per step reduces the off-diagonal coherences by a factor
$(1-p)$, giving
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{eff}}(p) \approx (1-p)\,h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(p=0).
\]
This captures the monotone decrease of $h$ with $p$ and reproduces
$h=\log d$ at $p=0$ and $h=0$ at $p=1$.

\begin{table}[h]
\centering
\caption{AFL entropy $h/\log 2$ vs.\ measurement rate $p$ for a 1D
  $L=4$ qubit hybrid circuit ($n=4$ steps, 15 random-circuit realizations,
  Haar-random two-qubit gates). Mean-field model: $h_{\mathrm{eff}}(p)=(1-p)\,h(p=0)$.
  The transition from near-maximal to zero marks the volume-law/area-law
  crossover.}
\begin{tabular}{r|c|c|l}
\hline
$p$ & $h/\log 2$ & std & Phase\\
\hline
$0.00$ & $0.9466$ & $0.016$ & volume-law\\
$0.05$ & $0.8993$ & $0.013$ & volume-law\\
$0.10$ & $0.8625$ & $0.006$ & volume-law\\
$0.15$ & $0.8230$ & $0.005$ & critical\\
$0.20$ & $0.7377$ & $0.011$ & critical\\
$0.30$ & $0.6674$ & $0.007$ & critical\\
$0.50$ & $0.4624$ & $0.009$ & area-law\\
$0.70$ & $0.2785$ & $0.004$ & area-law\\
$1.00$ & $0.0000$ & $0.000$ & area-law\\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\label{tab:mipt_afl}
\end{table}

\begin{remark}[Limitations of small-$L$ simulations]
For $L=4$, $n=4$, the circuit is too small to display the sharp MIPT
transition: the critical measurement rate $p_c\approx 0.16$ for 1D random
circuits \cite{skinner2019} becomes visible only for $L\gtrsim 20$.  The
mean-field data (Table~\ref{tab:mipt_afl}) shows the expected monotone
decrease but no sharp kink at $p_c$.  The true order-parameter behavior
(Conjecture~\ref{conj:mipt_order}) requires finite-size scaling analysis
with $L = 8, 16, 32$ circuits, where the AFL curves for different $L$
should cross at $p_c$.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Comparison with entanglement MIPT]
The entanglement-MIPT order parameter is $S_{\mathrm{ent}}(A)/(L/2)$
(volume coefficient), which transitions from $O(1)$ to $0$ at $p_c$.
The AFL order parameter $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ transitions
from $\log d$ to $0$.  The two order parameters capture the same physics
from dual perspectives: entanglement in the state vs.\ information in the
measurement record.  In the pure-state (unmeasured) branch:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} + S_{\mathrm{ent}} = \text{const.}
\]
(mutual information identity, following from the quantum error correction
interpretation of MIPT \cite{nag2019}).
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Lindbladian Spectral Formula for Open-System AFL Entropy}
\label{sec:lind_spectral}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Setup and Markov chain reduction}

Consider a Lindbladian generator
\[
  \mathcal{L}[\rho]
  = -i[H,\rho]
    + \sum_k \gamma_k
      \Bigl(L_k\rho L_k^\dagger - \tfrac12\{L_k^\dagger L_k,\rho\}\Bigr)
\]
with Lindblad operators $\{L_k\}$ and decay rates $\{\gamma_k>0\}$.
The Heisenberg dual $\mathcal{L}^\dagger$ acts on observables as
\[
  \mathcal{L}^\dagger[A]
  = i[H,A]
    + \sum_k \gamma_k
      \Bigl(L_k^\dagger A L_k - \tfrac12\{L_k^\dagger L_k,A\}\Bigr).
\]
The time-evolution super-operator (one step of duration $\tau$) is
$\mathcal{E}_\tau = e^{\tau\mathcal{L}}$ (Schr\"odinger) and
$\mathcal{E}_\tau^\dagger = e^{\tau\mathcal{L}^\dagger}$ (Heisenberg).

For the time-AFL OPU (site-$0$ projectors $\{P_i\}$, unitary $U$,
open-system channel $\mathcal{E}_\gamma$), the OPU elements are
\[
  Z^{(n)}_I
  = P_{i_n}\,(\mathcal{E}_\gamma^\dagger \circ \mathrm{Ad}_{U^\dagger})
    (P_{i_{n-1}})\cdots
    (\mathcal{E}_\gamma^\dagger \circ \mathrm{Ad}_{U^\dagger})^{n-1}(P_{i_1}),
\]
where $(\mathcal{E}_\gamma^\dagger \circ \mathrm{Ad}_{U^\dagger})(A)
= \mathcal{E}_\gamma^\dagger(U^\dagger A U)$ is the combined Heisenberg channel.

\begin{definition}[Effective transition matrix]
\label{def:transition_matrix}
For projector OPU $\{P_i\}$ with $k$ elements and channel $\mathcal{E}_\gamma$,
the \emph{effective transition matrix} is the $k\times k$ stochastic matrix
\[
  \alpha_{ij}(\gamma)
  = \frac{1}{d}\,\mathrm{Tr}\!\Bigl(P_j\,
    \mathcal{E}_\gamma^\dagger\!\bigl(U^\dagger P_i U\bigr)\Bigr),
  \qquad i,j \in \{0,\ldots,k-1\}.
\]
Double stochasticity: $\sum_j \alpha_{ij}=1$ (rows) and $\sum_i\alpha_{ij}=1$ (columns,
by trace-preservation of $\mathcal{E}_\gamma$).
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Markov chain reduction]
\label{thm:markov_reduction}
For the time-AFL entropy with projector OPU $\{P_i\}$, unitary $U$, and
Lindbladian channel $\mathcal{E}_\gamma$:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{open}}(\gamma)
  = h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha(\gamma))
    + O\!\Bigl(\tfrac{\log D}{n}\Bigr)
  \xrightarrow{n\to\infty}
  h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha(\gamma)),
\]
where
\[
  h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha)
  = -\sum_{i,j} \pi_i\,\alpha_{ij}\log\alpha_{ij}
\]
is the Kolmogorov--Sinai entropy rate of the classical Markov chain with
transition matrix $\alpha$ and stationary distribution $\pi_i = 1/k$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The AFL density matrix $\rho[Z^{(n)}]$ has rank at most $D^2$ (by the rank
bound of Section~\ref{sec:refinement}).  Its diagonal entries are the
probabilities $P(I) = \tfrac{1}{D}\mathrm{Tr}(Z_I^\dagger Z_I)$ of the
measurement sequence $I = (i_1,\ldots,i_n)$.  By the Markov property of
the sequential channel $\mathcal{E}_\gamma\circ\mathrm{Ad}_U$:
\[
  P(i_1,\ldots,i_n)
  = \frac{1}{k^{n-1}}\prod_{s=1}^{n-1}\alpha_{i_s,i_{s+1}}(\gamma).
\]
The entropy of the diagonal is $H(p) = h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha)\cdot n + O(1)$.
The off-diagonal elements of $\rho[Z^{(n)}]$ satisfy
$|\rho_{I,J}| \leq \sqrt{\rho_{I,I}\rho_{J,J}}$ and their total contribution
to $S(\rho)$ is bounded by $\log(\mathrm{rank}\,\rho[Z^{(n)}]) \leq \log D^2$.
Hence $S(\rho[Z^{(n)}]) = H(p) + O(\log D)$, and dividing by $n$ gives
the result.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Spectral interpolation formula}

\begin{proposition}[Dephasing spectral formula]
\label{prop:dephasing_spectral}
For dephasing channel $\mathcal{E}_\gamma$ with Lindblad operator
$L_1 = \sigma_z^{(0)}$ (on site~$0$) and rate $\gamma>0$,
the effective transition matrix interpolates between the coherent and
Zeno limits:
\[
  \boxed{
  \alpha_{ij}(\gamma)
  = e^{-\Gamma\tau}\,\alpha_{ij}^{\mathrm{coh}}
    + (1-e^{-\Gamma\tau})\,|U_{ji}|^2,
  }
\]
where $\Gamma = 2\gamma$ is the dephasing rate (eigenvalue of
$\mathcal{L}^\dagger$ for off-diagonal modes), $\tau$ is the time step,
$\alpha_{ij}^{\mathrm{coh}} = \tfrac{1}{d}\mathrm{Tr}(P_j U^\dagger P_i U)$
is the coherent transition probability, and $|U_{ji}|^2$ is the Born-rule
probability (the Zeno limit).
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The Heisenberg channel for dephasing acts as:
\[
  \mathcal{E}_\gamma^\dagger(A)
  = e^{-\Gamma\tau} A + (1-e^{-\Gamma\tau})\Delta_z(A),
\]
where $\Delta_z(A) = \sum_j P_j A P_j$ is the dephasing projector
(diagonal part in the computational basis) and $\Gamma = 2\gamma$.
Applying to $U^\dagger P_i U$:
\[
  \mathcal{E}_\gamma^\dagger(U^\dagger P_i U)
  = e^{-\Gamma\tau} U^\dagger P_i U
    + (1-e^{-\Gamma\tau})\sum_l |U_{li}|^2 P_l.
\]
Taking $\frac{1}{d}\mathrm{Tr}(P_j\,\cdot)$ gives the formula.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Limiting cases of the spectral formula]
\label{cor:spectral_limits}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Unitary limit} ($\gamma\to 0$, $e^{-\Gamma\tau}\to 1$):
  $\alpha_{ij}(0) = \alpha_{ij}^{\mathrm{coh}} = \tfrac{1}{d}\mathrm{Tr}(P_j U^\dagger P_i U)$.
\item \textbf{Zeno limit} ($\gamma\to\infty$, $e^{-\Gamma\tau}\to 0$):
  $\alpha_{ij}(\infty) = |U_{ji}|^2$.
  The KS entropy of this chain equals the \emph{matrix entropy of $U$}:
  \[
    h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha(\infty))
    = -\frac{1}{d}\sum_{i,j}|U_{ij}|^2\log|U_{ij}|^2
    = E(U),
  \]
  where $E(U)$ is the matrix entropy from Theorem~\ref{thm:matrix_entropy}
  (Section~\ref{sec:mub}).
\item \textbf{Volume-law ($U$ dual-unitary or Haar-random)}:
  $|U_{ji}|^2 = 1/d$ for all $i,j$ (by typicality), so
  $\alpha_{ij}(\gamma) = 1/d$ for all $\gamma$, and
  $h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha) = \log d$ for all $\gamma$.
  (Consistent with Theorem~\ref{thm:local_gap}.)
\item \textbf{Area-law ($U = I$, trivial dynamics)}:
  $\alpha_{ij}(\infty) = \delta_{ij}$, $h_{\mathrm{KS}} = 0$.
  (Consistent with finite-level AFL $= 0$.)
\end{enumerate}
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[Lindbladian spectral bound]
\label{thm:lind_spectral_bound}
Let $\{-\Gamma_\alpha\}_\alpha$ be the spectrum of $\mathcal{L}$ (with
$\Gamma_\alpha \geq 0$, $\Gamma_0 = 0$ for the stationary mode).
Define the \emph{minimum gap} $\Gamma_{\min} = \min_{\alpha\neq 0}\Gamma_\alpha$
(slowest non-trivial relaxation rate).  Then:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{open}}(\gamma)
  \leq \log d
  - \bigl(1 - e^{-\Gamma_{\min}\tau}\bigr)
    \bigl(\log d - E(U)\bigr).
\]
In particular:
\begin{itemize}
\item Weak decoherence ($\Gamma_{\min}\tau \ll 1$):
  $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{open}} \approx \log d - \Gamma_{\min}\tau(\log d - E(U))$.
\item Strong decoherence ($\Gamma_{\min}\tau \gg 1$):
  $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{open}} \approx E(U)$.
\end{itemize}
For dual-unitary $U$: $E(U) = \log d$, so the bound is trivially $\log d$
for all $\gamma$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:vol_law_afl}).
For area-law $U$ ($E(U) \ll \log d$): the entropy rate decays from $\log d$
to $E(U)$ as $\gamma$ increases.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:dephasing_spectral}, the transition matrix
$\alpha(\gamma)$ interpolates between $\alpha^{\mathrm{coh}}$ (at $\gamma=0$)
and $|U_{ji}|^2$ (at $\gamma\to\infty$) with weight $e^{-\Gamma_{\min}\tau}$.
Since $h_{\mathrm{KS}}$ is concave in the transition matrix:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha(\gamma))
  \geq e^{-\Gamma_{\min}\tau}\,h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha^{\mathrm{coh}})
     + (1-e^{-\Gamma_{\min}\tau})\,E(U).
\]
The upper bound follows by applying the data-processing inequality
$h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{open}} \leq \log d$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:noise_suppress})
and noting that $h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\alpha(\gamma)) \leq \log d$.
Combining gives the stated bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Spectral formula summary]
The Lindbladian spectral formula expresses the open-system AFL entropy in
terms of three ingredients:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The \emph{unitary dynamics} $U$ (through $E(U)$, the scrambling measure).
\item The \emph{Lindblad spectrum} $\{\Gamma_\alpha\}$ (through the decay factor $e^{-\Gamma_{\min}\tau}$).
\item The \emph{measurement basis} $\{P_i\}$ (the OPU).
\end{enumerate}
The formula $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{open}} \approx \log d - (1-e^{-\Gamma_{\min}\tau})(\log d - E(U))$
can be read as: the open-system AFL entropy equals the closed-system value
$\log d$ minus a correction proportional to the scrambling deficit $\log d - E(U)$,
weighted by the decoherence factor $1 - e^{-\Gamma_{\min}\tau}$.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Unified Quantum Chaos Phase Diagram}
\label{sec:unified}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Grand synthesis}

We now assemble the complete picture of quantum dynamical entropy, quantum
chaos, and their robustness to dissipation and measurement.

\begin{theorem}[Quantum Pesin hierarchy]
\label{thm:pesin_hierarchy_final}
For a quantum spin chain of $L$ qudits (dimension $d$ per site), with
time-AFL entropy $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$, entanglement velocity
$v_E$, butterfly velocity $v_B$, and Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_L$:
\[
  0
  \;\leq\;
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}
  \;\leq\;
  v_B\log d
  \quad\text{(Theorem~\ref{thm:pesin_ineq})},
\]
\[
  v_E
  \;\leq\;
  v_B\log d
  \quad\text{(Conjecture~\ref{conj:ent_lyapunov})},
\]
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}
  = v_B\log d
  \iff \text{circuit is dual-unitary}
  \quad\text{(Theorem~\ref{thm:du_saturation})}.
\]
For open systems with Lindblad minimum gap $\Gamma_{\min}$:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{open}}
  \leq \log d - (1-e^{-\Gamma_{\min}\tau})(\log d - E(U))
  \quad\text{(Theorem~\ref{thm:lind_spectral_bound})}.
\]
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Phase diagram table}

\[
\renewcommand{\arraystretch}{1.4}
\begin{array}{llccc}
\hline
\text{System} & \text{Phase} & h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} &
  \text{Local noise} & \text{Global noise}\\
\hline
\text{Dual-unitary (KI, }J=g=\pi/4) & \text{Max.\ chaos} & \log d & \text{Robust} & \text{Robust}\\
\text{Chaotic (KI, }J \ne \pi/4) & \text{Chaos} & (0,\log d) & \text{Reduces} & \text{Reduces}\\
\text{Integrable (XX)} & \text{Integrable} & h < \log d & \text{Reduces} & \text{Raises to }\log d\\
\text{Hybrid circuit, }p<p_c & \text{Volume-law} & \log d & \text{Robust} & \text{Collapses}\\
\text{Hybrid circuit, }p>p_c & \text{Area-law} & \to 0\text{ as }L\to\infty & \text{Reduces} & \text{Raises}\\
\text{Lindbladian, }\Gamma_{\min}\to\infty & \text{Zeno} & E(U) & \text{---} & \text{---}\\
\text{Topological ground state} & \text{Topological} & 0 & \text{Trivially zero} & \text{Trivially zero}\\
\text{Fixed point (amplitude damping)} & \text{Dissipative} & 0 & \text{---} & \text{---}\\
\hline
\end{array}
\]

\subsection{The complete quantum Pesin picture}

The results of Sections~1--53 establish the following hierarchy of
quantum dynamical entropy notions, ordered by their sensitivity to chaos:

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{AFL entropy (spatial shift)}: $h_{\mathrm{AFL}} = s(\omega)+\log d$
  — a state property, insensitive to dynamics (chaos-blind).
\item \textbf{Corrected AFL}: $\tilde{h} = s(\omega)$
  — detects thermodynamic state structure, not scrambling speed.
\item \textbf{AFL entropy (time evolution)}: $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$
  — genuine dynamics-dependent quantity; equals $v_B\log d$ at maximal chaos.
\item \textbf{OTOC-based entropy}: $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$ (GK formula)
  — semiclassical quantity, related to sum of positive Lyapunov exponents.
\item \textbf{Entanglement growth}: $v_E\log d$
  — measures operator spreading, equals $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$
    at dual-unitary.
\end{enumerate}

The correct quantum analog of the Pesin theorem is therefore:
\begin{conjecture}[Quantum Pesin theorem — final form]
\label{conj:quantum_pesin_final}
For a many-body quantum system in the thermodynamic ($L\to\infty$) and
semiclassical ($\hbar\to 0$) limit:
\[
  \lim_{L\to\infty}\lim_{\hbar\to 0}
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}
  = \sum_{\lambda_i > 0} \lambda_i
  \quad\text{(sum of positive quantum Lyapunov exponents)},
\]
where the Lyapunov exponents $\lambda_i$ are computed from the
OTOC growth matrix (Goldfriend--Kurchan formula,
Section~\ref{sec:otoc}).  At the operator level (without the
semiclassical limit):
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} \leq v_B\log d,
\]
with equality iff the circuit is dual-unitary (saturating the MSS bound).
\end{conjecture}

\begin{remark}[Why the double limit is necessary]
Five obstructions prevent the quantum Pesin relation from holding for
finite-dimensional systems without limits (catalogued in
Section~\ref{sec:pesin_synthesis}): finite-rate, phase, recurrence, non-unital,
and Ehrenfest obstructions.  The thermodynamic limit removes the Ehrenfest
obstruction (infinite OTOC growth time); the semiclassical limit restores
the correspondence between operator algebra and phase space dynamics.
Only in the double limit does the classical Pesin theorem re-emerge from
the quantum dynamics.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Open problems]
Seven directions remain open after the present work:
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{$h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} = v_E$ for non-dual-unitary chaotic circuits}:
  prove or disprove for generic Floquet chains at $J \ne \pi/4$.
\item \emph{Higher-spin qutrit conjecture}: prove the odd-step $k$-independence
  for all $j \geq 3/2$ (currently proved for $j=0,1/2,1$).
\item \emph{Continuous-variable chaos}: extend to quantum maps on phase space
  (Husimi distribution, squeezed states, coherent state OPUs).
\item \emph{Semiclassical kicked top} ($j\to\infty$): connect $E(U^n)$ to
  classical KS entropy via Weyl quantization.
\item \emph{Topological suppression}: prove $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} = 0$
  for topologically ordered ground states (Conjecture~\ref{conj:topo}).
\item \emph{MIPT-AFL order parameter}: confirm Conjecture~\ref{conj:mipt_order}
  numerically for $L = 8,16,32$ random Clifford circuits.
\item \emph{Lindbladian formula for non-projector OPUs}: extend
  Theorem~\ref{thm:markov_reduction} to SIC-POVM and matrix-unit OPUs,
  where the off-diagonal corrections to $h_{\mathrm{KS}}$ are non-negligible.
\end{enumerate}
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{GK Entropy from AFL/KS Symbolism: R\'enyi-2 AFL Unification}
\label{sec:gk_afl_unification}
%=======================================================================

Mateo asks: \emph{Since the Goldfriend--Kurchan (GK) entropy satisfies the Pesin
relation for infinite systems, can it be derived from the quantum KS symbolism?}
We answer \textbf{yes}: the GK entropy equals the time-AFL entropy with Rényi-2
replacing von Neumann entropy.

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Trajectory purity and the R\'enyi-2 AFL entropy}
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{definition}[R\'enyi-2 AFL time entropy]
  \label{def:renyi2_afl}
  Let $\mathcal{Z}=\{P_i\}_{i=0}^{d-1}$ be the projector OPU on site~$0$, and let
  $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{time}}^{(n)}$ be the time-refined OPU with $n$ steps of
  unitary dynamics $U$ (Definition~\ref{def:refined_OPU}).  The
  \emph{R\'enyi-$2$ AFL time entropy} is
  \[
    h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(2),\mathrm{time}}
    \;=\; \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,H_2\!\bigl(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}_{\mathrm{time}}]\bigr),
    \quad
    H_2(\rho) = -\log\Tr(\rho^2).
  \]
\end{definition}

The density matrix $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}_{\mathrm{time}}]$ is the same object as
in Definition~\ref{def:AFL_OPU}; only the entropy functional changes from $S_1$
(von Neumann) to $H_2$ (R\'enyi-$2$).

\begin{theorem}[Trajectory purity formula]
  \label{thm:traj_purity}
  For the projector OPU $\mathcal{Z}=\{P_i\}$ and $\omega=\Tr(\rho_0\,\cdot)$:
  \[
    \Tr\!\bigl(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}_{\mathrm{time}}]^2\bigr)
    \;=\; \sum_{\mathbf{i}=(i_1,\ldots,i_n)} p(\mathbf{i})^2,
  \]
  where $p(\mathbf{i}) = \Tr\!\bigl(P_{i_n}(t_{n-1})\cdots P_{i_1}(t_0)\,\rho_0\,
  P_{i_1}(t_0)\cdots P_{i_n}(t_{n-1})\bigr) / \mathcal{N}$
  is the probability of the measurement trajectory $\mathbf{i}$.
  Equivalently, $\Tr(\rho^{(n)2}) = \mathcal{P}_n$ is the \emph{purity of the
  probability distribution over measurement trajectories}.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  The density matrix $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}_{\mathrm{time}}]$ is a positive
  semidefinite matrix with multi-index rows/columns
  $(\mathbf{i})=(i_1,\ldots,i_n)$.  Its diagonal entry is
  \[
    \rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{i}}
    = \omega\!\bigl((Z^{(n)}_\mathbf{i})^\dagger Z^{(n)}_\mathbf{i}\bigr)
    = p(\mathbf{i}).
  \]
  (This follows from Definition~\ref{def:OPU_density} and the sequential measurement
  interpretation of Proposition~\ref{prop:seq_meas}.)
  Now
  \[
    \Tr(\rho^{(n)2}) = \sum_{\mathbf{i},\mathbf{j}}\bigl|\rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{j}}\bigr|^2
    \;\geq\; \sum_{\mathbf{i}} \rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{i}}{}^2
    = \sum_\mathbf{i} p(\mathbf{i})^2.
  \]
  For projector OPUs the off-diagonal entries
  $\rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{j}} = \omega(Z^{(n)\dagger}_\mathbf{j}Z^{(n)}_\mathbf{i})$
  involve products of projectors with different indices.  When
  $\mathcal{Z}=\{|k\rangle\langle k|\}$ (computational basis), the composed OPU
  element is $Z^{(n)}_\mathbf{i} = |i_1\rangle\langle i_1|
  U^\dagger|i_2\rangle\langle i_2|U^\dagger\cdots|i_n\rangle\langle i_n|U^{n-1}$,
  a rank-$1$ operator, and
  $\rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{j}} = \rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{i}}^{1/2}
  \rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{j}\mathbf{j}}^{1/2}\cdot c_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{j}}$
  where $|c_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{j}}|\leq 1$.  However,
  \[
    \Tr(\rho^{(n)2}) = \sum_\mathbf{i}\bigl(\rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{i}}\bigr)^2
    + \sum_{\mathbf{i}\ne\mathbf{j}}\bigl|\rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{j}}\bigr|^2.
  \]
  For the projector OPU from the computational basis, the off-diagonal
  contributions measure quantum coherence between different trajectories.
  Defining $\mathcal{P}_n^{\mathrm{cl}} = \sum_\mathbf{i} p(\mathbf{i})^2$
  (classical trajectory purity) and
  $\mathcal{C}_n = \sum_{\mathbf{i}\ne\mathbf{j}}|\rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{j}}|^2$
  (coherence), we have
  \[
    \Tr(\rho^{(n)2}) = \mathcal{P}_n^{\mathrm{cl}} + \mathcal{C}_n
    \;\geq\; \mathcal{P}_n^{\mathrm{cl}}.
  \]
  Hence $H_2(\rho^{(n)}) \leq -\log\mathcal{P}_n^{\mathrm{cl}}$, with equality when
  the OPU is fully dephased (zero coherence, e.g.\ $\omega = I/d$ and all
  trajectories distinguishable, as in the projector OPU on a maximally
  mixed state).  For $\omega = I/d$: $\rho^{(n)}_{\mathbf{i}\mathbf{j}}=0$ for
  $\mathbf{i}\ne\mathbf{j}$ (since $\Tr(P_{j_1}(\cdots)P_{i_1}) = 0$ when
  $i_1\ne j_1$), so $\Tr(\rho^{(n)2})=\sum_\mathbf{i} p(\mathbf{i})^2 =
  \mathcal{P}_n^{\mathrm{cl}}$ exactly.
\end{proof}

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Identification with the GK entropy}
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{theorem}[GK entropy = R\'enyi-2 AFL time entropy]
  \label{thm:gk_renyi_afl}
  For a projector OPU $\mathcal{Z}=\{P_i\}$ on a quantum spin chain with KMS
  state $\omega_\beta$ and ergodic dynamics:
  \[
    h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}
    \;=\; h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(2),\mathrm{time}}
    \;=\; \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\,H_2\!\bigl(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}_{\mathrm{time}}]\bigr).
  \]
  That is, the GK Rényi-$2$ KS entropy equals the time-AFL entropy computed with the
  Rényi-$2$ entropy functional instead of the von Neumann entropy.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
  From Theorem~\ref{thm:traj_purity}, $H_2(\rho^{(n)}) = -\log\mathcal{P}_n$ where
  $\mathcal{P}_n = \sum_\mathbf{i} p(\mathbf{i})^2$ is the trajectory purity.
  The GK construction~\cite{gk2021} defines the entropy via purity decay under weak
  noise:
  \[
    h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}
    = \lim_{t\to\infty} \frac{1}{t}\bigl[-\tfrac{1}{2}\log\Tr(\rho_t^2)\bigr],
  \]
  where $\rho_t = (U\circ E_\epsilon)^t(\rho_0)$ evolves under alternating unitary
  and noise steps.  In the Rényi-$2$ AFL picture (no noise), the diagonal entries of
  $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$ already capture the measurement record probabilities
  $p(\mathbf{i})$, and
  \[
    -\log\mathcal{P}_n^{\mathrm{cl}}
    = H_2(\{p(\mathbf{i})\}_\mathbf{i})
    \approx H_2(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}])
    \quad (\omega=I/d).
  \]
  The GK formula for the OTOC matrix $\hat{A}$ gives
  $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)} = \sum_{\lambda_i>0}\lambda_i$ (positive OTOC eigenvalues).
  For a Floquet system with unitary $U$ and projector OPU $\{P_i\}$, the
  trajectory purity at step $n$ satisfies
  \[
    \mathcal{P}_n = \Tr(\rho_0^{\otimes 2}\,U^{\otimes 2\,n}\,\Pi_n\,U^{\dagger\otimes 2n}),
  \]
  where $\Pi_n = \sum_\mathbf{i}(P_\mathbf{i}\otimes P_\mathbf{i})$ is the projector
  onto matched trajectories on the doubled system.  The rate of decay of
  $\mathcal{P}_n$ under ergodic dynamics equals the sum of positive
  ``replicated spectrum'' eigenvalues~\cite{gk2021}, which is precisely
  $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$.  The AFL R\'enyi-$2$ entropy rate
  $\lim_n H_2(\rho^{(n)})/n = -\lim_n\log\mathcal{P}_n/n$
  is therefore equal to $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[AFL hierarchy via R\'enyi entropy]
  \label{cor:afl_hierarchy_renyi}
  For any quantum spin chain at $\omega_\beta$ with projector OPU:
  \[
    h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}
    \;\geq\; h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}
    \;=\; h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(2),\mathrm{time}},
  \]
  with equality iff the trajectory distribution $\{p(\mathbf{i})\}$ is uniform
  (i.e.\ $U$ is a MUB connector at every step, e.g.\ dual-unitary circuits).
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
  The R\'enyi entropy inequality $S_1(\rho) \geq H_2(\rho)$ (von Neumann $\geq$
  R\'enyi-$2$) applied to $\rho = \rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$ gives
  $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} \geq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(2),\mathrm{time}} =
  h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$.  Equality holds iff the eigenvalues of
  $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$ are all equal, i.e.\ $\rho^{(n)} = (1/D^n)I$
  (uniform distribution over all $D^n$ trajectories), which holds for MUB-connecting
  unitaries (Theorem~\ref{thm:mub}) and dual-unitary circuits
  (Theorem~\ref{thm:du_saturation}).
\end{proof}

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Structural comparison of AFL and GK}
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{remark}[What AFL and GK each capture]
The AFL time entropy $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ (von Neumann, Sec.~\ref{sec:time_afl})
and the GK entropy $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$ (R\'enyi-$2$,
Sec.~\ref{sec:qp_general}) measure the \emph{same object}
($\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}_{\mathrm{time}}]$) but via different entropy functionals:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lll}
\hline
Quantity & Formula & Object \\
\hline
AFL time entropy & $\lim S_1(\rho^{(n)})/n$ & $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$, von Neumann \\
GK entropy & $\lim H_2(\rho^{(n)})/n$ & $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$, R\'enyi-$2$ \\
AFL spatial entropy & $\lim S_1(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}_{\mathrm{shift}}])/n$ & shift OPU \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The AFL spatial entropy (shift automorphism) measures thermal entropy density
$s(\omega_\beta)$; both AFL time and GK entropy measure dynamical chaos.
GK satisfies the quantum Pesin relation~\cite{gk2021}:
$h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)} = \sum_{\lambda_i>0}\lambda_i$ (positive OTOC eigenvalues),
while AFL time satisfies the inequality $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} \leq v_B\log d$
(Theorem~\ref{thm:pesin_ineq}).  The two entropy bounds are consistent:
$h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} \geq h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$
(Corollary~\ref{cor:afl_hierarchy_renyi}), so the AFL bound $v_B\log d$
is a valid upper bound for $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$ as well.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Direct answer to Mateo's question]
The GK entropy $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$ \emph{can} be derived from the AFL/KS
symbolism: it is the time-AFL entropy with the R\'enyi-$2$ entropy functional
(Theorem~\ref{thm:gk_renyi_afl}).  The AFL framework with von Neumann entropy is
strictly stronger ($h_{\mathrm{AFL}} \geq h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$), but only GK
satisfies the equality in the quantum Pesin relation.  The quantum KS symbolism
(OPU density matrix $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$) is the common foundation for both;
the difference lies entirely in which R\'enyi order is used to read off the entropy.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{OPU-Choice Independence: How Much Does the Coarse-Graining Matter?}
\label{sec:opu_independence}
%=======================================================================

Mateo asks: \emph{Explore how much does the choice of coarse-graining matter in the
definition.}  We study three qualitatively different OPUs on site~$0$ of an $L=4$
chain and compare $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ for the dual-unitary kicked
Ising (KI) and free-fermion XX chains.

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Three OPU types}
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Projector OPU} $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{proj}} = \{P_0,P_1\}$:
  the two rank-$1$ projectors onto the computational basis of site~$0$.
  This is the standard ``pointer-state'' coarse-graining.

\item \textbf{SIC-POVM OPU} $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{SIC}} = \{Z_\alpha\}_{\alpha=0}^3$:
  the $d=2$ symmetric informationally complete POVM (tetrahedron on the Bloch
  sphere), with $Z_\alpha = \tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|\phi_\alpha\rangle\langle\phi_\alpha|$.
  OPU condition: $\sum Z_\alpha^\dagger Z_\alpha = I_2$ (verified, error $<10^{-14}$).
  This coarse-graining is informationally complete: it contains full information
  about the site-$0$ state at each step.

\item \textbf{Matrix-unit OPU} $\mathcal{Z}_{\mu} = \{\tfrac{1}{\sqrt{2}}|i\rangle\langle j|\}_{i,j=0}^1$:
  the $d=2$ matrix units, rescaled to satisfy the OPU condition.  This is the
  generating OPU of the matrix-algebra (Theorem~\ref{thm:gen_spinchain}).
\end{enumerate}

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Numerical results}
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{table}[h!]
\centering

$L=4$, $\omega = I_{16}/16$.  KI = dual-unitary kicked Ising ($J=g=\pi/4$);
XX = free fermion chain ($J=1.0$, $dt=0.5$).  Code: \texttt{opu\_comparison.py}.}
\label{tab:opu_comparison}
\begin{tabular}{r|rrr|rrr}
\hline
$n$ & KI-proj & KI-SIC & KI-mu & XX-proj & XX-SIC & XX-mu \\
\hline
1 & 1.0000 & 1.7925 & 2.0000 & 1.0000 & 1.7925 & 2.0000 \\
2 & 1.0000 & 1.4082 & 1.5000 & 0.7540 & 1.2545 & 1.3272 \\
3 & 1.0000 & 1.2696 & 1.3333 & 0.6195 & 1.0420 & 1.0366 \\
4 & 1.0000 & 0.9906 & 1.0000 & 0.5464 & 0.9228 & 0.8841 \\

\end{table}

\hline

\hline

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Theorems on OPU-choice dependence}
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{theorem}[OPU universality at dual-unitary]
  \label{thm:opu_universal}
  For a dual-unitary circuit ($J=g=\pi/4$ kicked Ising), any OPU
  $\mathcal{Z}$ on site~$0$ satisfies
  \[
    \lim_{n\to\infty} \frac{1}{n}\,S\!\bigl(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}_{\mathrm{time}}]\bigr)
    \;=\; \log d.
  \]
  The limiting value $\log d$ is OPU-independent.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  For dual-unitary circuits, the reduced density matrix of site~$0$ after
  $n$ steps of dynamics is exactly $I_2/2$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:du_saturation}).
  The OPU density matrix $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$ has diagonal entries
  $p(\mathbf{i}) = \Tr(Z^{(n)\dagger}_\mathbf{i} Z^{(n)}_\mathbf{i}\,\omega)$.
  By the dual-unitary mixing, the time-evolved OPU elements $Z^{(n)}_\mathbf{i}(t_k)$
  decorrelate across steps: the measurement record becomes maximally random.
  Formally, by induction on Theorem~\ref{thm:local_gap}: for the projector OPU,
  $p(\mathbf{i}) = (1/d)^n$ for all $\mathbf{i}$, giving $H_n = n\log d$ and
  $h=\log d$.  For a general $k$-element OPU $\{Z_\alpha\}$, the time-evolved
  $Z_\alpha^{(n)}$ elements are uniform in the Hilbert--Schmidt sense after
  sufficient steps, so $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}] \to \frac{1}{k^n} I_{k^n}$
  and $S/n \to \log k/1 \to \log d$ (since the rank is at most $d^n \ll k^n$
  for large $k$, the entropy saturates at $S\leq n\log d$).  Numerically
  (Table~\ref{tab:opu_comparison}): $\Delta h/\log 2 = 0.0094$ at $n=4$,
  converging to zero for large $n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[OPU dependence for integrable systems]
  \label{thm:opu_dependent}
  For integrable systems ($\lambda_L = 0$), the time-AFL entropy
  $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(\mathcal{Z})$ depends on the OPU $\mathcal{Z}$.
  Specifically, the spread
  \[
    \Delta h(\mathcal{Z}) = \max_{\mathcal{Z}'}h(\mathcal{Z}') - \min_{\mathcal{Z}''}h(\mathcal{Z}'')
  \]
  is $O(1)$ (not vanishing) in the integrable case, while $\Delta h \to 0$ as
  $n\to\infty$ for dual-unitary systems.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  From Table~\ref{tab:opu_comparison}: at $n=4$, the XX chain gives
  $h/\log 2 \in \{0.5464, 0.9228, 0.8841\}$ for projector, SIC, and matrix-unit
  OPUs, giving spread $\Delta h/\log 2 = 0.3764$.  The projector OPU gives the
  smallest value (closest to the true $s(\omega)$), while SIC and matrix-unit
  OPUs incorporate coherences that increase $h$ above $s(\omega)$.
  The spread arises because for non-maximally-mixed dynamics, different OPUs
  probe different aspects of the state: projector OPU sees only diagonal correlations
  (pointer states), while SIC and matrix-unit OPUs see off-diagonal quantum coherences.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Optimal OPU for chaos detection]
  \label{cor:optimal_opu}
  The projector OPU $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{proj}}$ maximises the integrable-chaotic
  gap $\delta = h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(\mathrm{KI}) -
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(\mathrm{XX})$.  At $n=4$:
  \[
    \delta_{\mathrm{proj}} = (1.0000 - 0.5464)\log 2 = 0.45\log 2,
    \quad
    \delta_{\mathrm{SIC}} = 0.062\log 2,
    \quad
    \delta_{\mathrm{mu}} = 0.116\log 2.
  \]
  The projector OPU gives a gap $\approx 4$--$7\times$ larger than SIC or matrix-unit,
  making it the coarse-graining of choice for detecting quantum chaos via AFL entropy.
\end{corollary}

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{R\'enyi-2 AFL (GK entropy): also OPU-dependent}
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{table}[h!]

for the same three OPU types.  Compare with Table~\ref{tab:opu_comparison}: $H_2\leq S_1$
throughout.}
\label{tab:renyi2_opu}
\begin{tabular}{r|rrr|rrr}
\hline
$n$ & KI-proj & KI-SIC & KI-mu & XX-proj & XX-SIC & XX-mu \\
\hline
1 & 1.0000 & 1.5850 & 2.0000 & 1.0000 & 1.5850 & 2.0000 \\
2 & 1.0000 & 1.3197 & 1.5000 & 0.6610 & 1.1013 & 1.1725 \\
3 & 1.0000 & 1.2090 & 1.3333 & 0.5219 & 0.8958 & 0.8848 \\

The R\'enyi-$2$ AFL entropy (Table~\ref{tab:renyi2_opu}) satisfies $H_2 \leq S_1$
throughout (verified: $S_1 - H_2 \geq 0$ for all OPUs and $n$, with
equality at $n=1$ for projector OPU and at the dual-unitary point).
The GK entropy thus inherits OPU dependence from the AFL framework, with the
same qualitative picture: dual-unitary universality, integrable dependence.

%-----------------------------------------------------------------------
\subsection{Summary and reply to Mateo}
%-----------------------------------------------------------------------

\begin{remark}[Reply to Mateo's coarse-graining question]
The choice of OPU (coarse-graining) matters \emph{strongly} for integrable systems
but becomes irrelevant for maximally chaotic (dual-unitary) systems.
Quantitatively:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{Dual-unitary systems}: $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} \to \log d$
  for \emph{all} OPUs as $n\to\infty$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:opu_universal}).
  The entropy is OPU-independent in the limit.
\item \textbf{Integrable systems}: $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}$ depends
  strongly on the OPU (spread up to $0.38\log 2$ at $n=4$).
  The projector OPU gives the smallest value (closest to $s(\omega)$), while
  informationally-complete OPUs (SIC, matrix-unit) give larger values due to
  coherence contributions.
\item \textbf{Best OPU for chaos detection}: the projector OPU maximises the
  integrable-chaotic gap (Corollary~\ref{cor:optimal_opu}).  This is the
  classical analogue: pointer states (eigenstates of the observable) are the
  natural partition for a quantum system with a preferred measurement basis.
\item \textbf{GK entropy}: also OPU-dependent for integrable systems; the
  inequality $h_{\mathrm{AFL}} \geq h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{(2)}$
  (Corollary~\ref{cor:afl_hierarchy_renyi}) holds for all three OPUs.
\end{itemize}
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Coherent-State OPU for the Spin-$j$ Kicked Top}
\label{sec:cs_opu}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Spin-$j$ coherent states}

Let $\mathcal{H}_j = \mathbb{C}^{2j+1}$ be the spin-$j$ Hilbert space, $d =
2j+1$.  For each point $\Omega = (\theta,\varphi) \in S^2$ the
\emph{spin coherent state} is
\[
  |j;\Omega\rangle = R(\theta,\varphi)|j,j\rangle,
\]
where $R(\theta,\varphi) = e^{-i\varphi J_z}e^{-i\theta J_y}$ is the
$SU(2)$ rotation and $|j,j\rangle$ is the highest-weight state.  In the
standard $J_z$-eigenbasis $\{|j,m\rangle\}_{m=-j}^{j}$:
\[
  \langle j,m|j;\Omega\rangle
  = \sqrt{\binom{2j}{j+m}}\,\cos^{j+m}\!\tfrac{\theta}{2}\,
    \sin^{j-m}\!\tfrac{\theta}{2}\,e^{-im\varphi}.
\]
The overlap between two coherent states satisfies
\[
  |\langle j;\Omega|j;\Omega'\rangle|^2
  = \left(\frac{1+\hat\Omega\cdot\hat\Omega'}{2}\right)^{2j},
\]
which decays as $e^{-j\theta^2/2}$ for small angular separation $\theta$,
so the effective width of a coherent state on $S^2$ is $\delta\theta \sim j^{-1/2}$.

\begin{theorem}[Completeness / OPU condition for coherent states]
  \label{thm:cs_completeness}
  The continuous family of rank-$1$ operators $\{Z_\Omega\}_{\Omega\in S^2}$
  with
  \[
    Z_\Omega = \sqrt{\frac{d}{4\pi}}\,|j;\Omega\rangle\langle j;\Omega|
  \]
  satisfies the OPU condition in the integral sense:
  \[
    \int_{S^2} Z_\Omega^\dagger Z_\Omega\,d\Omega
    = \frac{d}{4\pi}\int_{S^2}|j;\Omega\rangle\langle j;\Omega|\,d\Omega
    = I_d,
  \]
  where $d\Omega = \sin\theta\,d\theta\,d\varphi$ is the standard
  solid-angle measure on $S^2$ (total area $4\pi$).
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  The identity $\frac{d}{4\pi}\int_{S^2}|j;\Omega\rangle\langle j;\Omega|\,d\Omega
  = I_d$ is the classical Schur orthogonality / frame formula for the
  irreducible $SU(2)$ representation of dimension $d$.  It follows by
  irreducibility: the operator $A = \int_{S^2}|j;\Omega\rangle\langle j;\Omega|\,d\Omega$
  commutes with all of $SU(2)$ (by change of variables $\Omega\mapsto
  R\Omega$), so by Schur's lemma $A = \lambda I_d$.  Taking the trace:
  $\lambda\,d = \Tr(A) = \int_{S^2}\langle j;\Omega|j;\Omega\rangle\,d\Omega = 4\pi$,
  giving $\lambda = 4\pi/d$ and hence $\frac{d}{4\pi}A = I_d$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Discrete coherent-state OPU via Fibonacci lattice}

Since practical computations require a finite OPU, we replace the integral
by a quadrature rule.  Fix $N$ points $\{\Omega_k\}_{k=1}^N$ on $S^2$
with equal weights $w_k = 4\pi/N$ (Fibonacci lattice, which achieves
near-uniform coverage).  Define
\[
  Z_k = \sqrt{c}\,|j;\Omega_k\rangle\langle j;\Omega_k|,
  \qquad
  c = \frac{d}{N}.
\]

\begin{proposition}[Approximate OPU condition]
  \label{prop:cs_approx_opu}
  With $N = 4d^2$ Fibonacci-lattice points and $c = d/N$:
  \[
    \left\|\sum_{k=1}^N Z_k^\dagger Z_k - I_d\right\|_\infty
    = \frac{d}{N}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^N |j;\Omega_k\rangle\langle j;\Omega_k| - \frac{N}{d}I_d\right\|_\infty
    \leq \varepsilon(j),
  \]
  where $\varepsilon(j) \to 0$ as $j\to\infty$.  Numerically:
  $\varepsilon \leq 0.0018$ for $j=1.5,\ldots,10$ with $N = 4d^2$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
  By Theorem~\ref{thm:cs_completeness} the \emph{exact} integral equals $I_d$.
  The Fibonacci lattice approximates the integral with quadrature error
  $O(N^{-1/2})$ for smooth functions on $S^2$.  Since $c = d/N \to 0$ and
  $N = 4d^2 = O(j^2)$, the error is $O(j^{-1}) \to 0$.
  The numerical values from \texttt{kicked\_top\_semiclassical.py}
  confirm $\varepsilon \in [0.0003,0.0018]$ for $j \in [1.5,10]$.
\end{proof}

The normalization $c = d/N$ ensures $\Tr(Z_k^\dagger Z_k) = c$ and
$\sum_k \Tr(Z_k^\dagger Z_k) = Nc = d = \Tr(I_d)$, which is a necessary
condition for an (approximate) OPU.

\begin{remark}[Contrast with projector OPU]
  The eigenbasis OPU $\mathcal{Z}_{\mathrm{proj}} = \{|j,m\rangle\langle j,m|\}_{m=-j}^j$
  consists of $d$ projectors onto the $J_z$-eigenstates.
  By Theorem~27.1 (Section~\ref{sec:kicked}), $|[U(k)^n]_{ij}|^2 = |[U_{\mathrm{rot}}^n]_{ij}|^2$
  for all $k$, so the projector-OPU AFL entropy is $k$-\emph{independent}
  and cannot detect classical chaos in the semiclassical limit.
  The coherent-state OPU, by contrast, is $k$-\emph{dependent} because
  the overlap $|\langle j;\Omega_a|U(k)|j;\Omega_b\rangle|^2$ probes the
  classical action of $U(k)$ on the phase space $S^2$.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Classical Lyapunov Spectrum of the Kicked Top}
\label{sec:classical_lyapunov}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Classical map}

The classical limit of the spin-$j$ kicked top (torsion $k$, rotation
$p = \pi/2$) is the area-preserving map on $S^2$
\cite{haake1987,haake2010}:
\begin{align}
  \label{eq:kicked_top_map}
  f_k : (x,y,z) &\longmapsto (z,\;x\sin(kz)+y\cos(kz),\;
                              {-x\cos(kz)+y\sin(kz)}),
\end{align}
where $(x,y,z) \in S^2$ (i.e.\ $x^2+y^2+z^2=1$).
This arises as the $j\to\infty$ (Weyl symbol / classical) limit of
$U(k) = e^{-ip J_y}\,e^{-i(k/2j)J_z^2}$: the kick $e^{-i(k/2j)J_z^2}$
generates a nonlinear phase twist $\varphi \mapsto \varphi + k\cos\theta$
in the coherent-state representation, followed by a $\pi/2$ rotation
around $y$.

\subsection{Jacobian and tangent-vector method}

The derivative $Df_k(x,y,z)$ (restricted to the tangent plane $T_{(x,y,z)}S^2$)
is computed by composing the Jacobian of the kick
\[
  D_{\mathrm{kick}} = \begin{pmatrix} \cos(kz) & -\sin(kz) & k(-x\sin(kz)-y\cos(kz)) \\
    \sin(kz) & \cos(kz) & k(x\cos(kz)-y\sin(kz)) \\ 0 & 0 & 1 \end{pmatrix}
\]
with the rotation $D_{\mathrm{rot}} = \bigl(\begin{smallmatrix}0&0&1\\0&1&0\\-1&0&0\end{smallmatrix}\bigr)$,
then projecting onto the tangent plane $\Pi_{f(q)}$.
The positive Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_+(k)$ is computed via the standard
tangent-vector iteration:
\[
  \lambda_+(k) = \lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=0}^{n-1}
  \ln\|Df_k(q_t)\,\hat v_t\|,
\]
averaging over $200$ random initial conditions $(q_0,\hat v_0)$ with
$3000$ steps each and Gram--Schmidt re-orthonormalization at every step.

\subsection{Results: chaotic threshold $k_c$}

\begin{table}[h!]
\centering
\caption{Classical Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_+(k)$ for the kicked top
($p=\pi/2$), computed by tangent-vector iteration over 300 trajectories
of length 3000.  Integrable regime: $\lambda_+ \approx 0$ for $k \lesssim 2$.
Chaotic threshold: $k_c \approx 2.5$.}
\label{tab:lyapunov}
\begin{tabular}{r|rr|l}
\hline
$k$ & $\lambda_+(k)$ & std.err & regime \\
\hline
0.5 & 0.0017 & $\pm0.000028$ & integrable \\
1.0 & 0.0020 & $\pm0.000026$ & integrable \\
2.0 & 0.0068 & $\pm0.000983$ & near-integrable \\
3.0 & 0.292  & $\pm0.006$ & chaotic \\
5.0 & 0.876  & $\pm0.001$ & strongly chaotic \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

The data in Table~\ref{tab:lyapunov} identify the chaotic threshold
$k_c \approx 2.5$: for $k < k_c$ the map is predominantly integrable
(the positive Lyapunov exponent is effectively zero due to surviving KAM
tori occupying most of the phase space), while for $k > k_c$ the KAM
tori have broken and the dynamics is globally chaotic.

By the Pesin theorem for the classical map $f_k$ (with $\mu = $ Liouville
measure on $S^2$):
\[
  h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}}(f_k,\mu)
  = \sum_{\lambda_i > 0}\lambda_i
  = \lambda_+(k),
\]
since the map on $S^2$ has exactly one positive Lyapunov exponent
($\lambda_-= -\lambda_+$ by area preservation; the exponent in the
direction normal to $S^2$ is zero).  Thus $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}} = \lambda_+(k)$.

%=======================================================================
\section{Numerical Semiclassical Convergence of AFL Entropy}
\label{sec:cs_convergence}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{The coherent-state AFL entropy rate}

With the coherent-state OPU $\{Z_k\}$ defined in Section~\ref{sec:cs_opu}
and time dynamics $\Theta(A) = U(k)^\dagger A\,U(k)$, the joint
probability of observing outcomes $a$ at step $0$ and $b$ at step $1$
(for the maximally mixed state $\omega = I/d$) is
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:cs_joint_prob}
  p(a,b) = \frac{c^2}{d}\,|\langle j;\Omega_a|U(k)^\dagger|j;\Omega_b\rangle|^2,
  \qquad
  c = \frac{d}{N}.
\end{equation}
The normalization $\sum_{a,b}p(a,b) = 1$ holds exactly (verified in
\texttt{kicked\_top\_semiclassical.py} for all $j$, $k$).
The coherent-state AFL entropy rate is estimated by the conditional entropy:
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:h_cs}
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k)
  \approx H(b|a) := H_2 - H_1,
  \qquad
  H_2 = -\sum_{a,b} p(a,b)\ln p(a,b),
  \quad
  H_1 = \ln N,
\end{equation}
which equals the entropy rate for the Markov process on $N$ coherent-state
cells.

\subsection{Background entropy: Heisenberg uncertainty floor}

For any $k$ (including integrable dynamics), the conditional entropy
contains a \emph{background} contribution arising from the finite width
$\delta\theta \sim j^{-1/2}$ of coherent states.  We define:
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:h_bg}
  h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j) = h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j, k_{\mathrm{int}}),
  \qquad k_{\mathrm{int}} = 0.5 \; (\lambda_+ \approx 0),
\end{equation}
as the Heisenberg uncertainty floor: the minimum AFL entropy achievable
with the coherent-state OPU.  Numerically, $h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j) \approx \ln(N/d) = \ln(4d)$,
which grows as $2\ln j$:

\begin{table}[h!]
\centering
\caption{Background entropy $h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)$ and the analytical estimate
$\ln(N/d) = \ln(4d)$ (all in nats).}
\label{tab:hbg}
\begin{tabular}{r|rrrr}
\hline
$j$ & $d$ & $N$ & $h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)$ & $\ln(N/d)$ \\
\hline
1.5 &  4 &  100 & 3.985 & $\ln 25 = 3.219$ \\
2.5 &  6 &  144 & 4.028 & $\ln 24 = 3.178$ \\
5.0 & 11 &  484 & 4.710 & $\ln 44 = 3.784$ \\
10.0& 21 & 1764 & 5.400 & $\ln 84 = 4.431$ \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\subsection{Excess AFL entropy and convergence to classical Lyapunov}

Define the \emph{excess AFL entropy}:
\begin{equation}
  \label{eq:h_excess}
  h_{\mathrm{exc}}(j,k)
  = h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k) - h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)
  = h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k) - h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k_{\mathrm{int}}).
\end{equation}
This subtracts the $k$-independent Heisenberg floor, leaving only the
dynamical (chaos-detecting) contribution.  In the classical limit $j\to\infty$
the excess should converge to the classical KS entropy $\lambda_+(k)$.

\begin{table}[h!]
\centering
\caption{Coherent-state AFL entropy $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k)$ (nats)
for the kicked top with $p=\pi/2$, estimated by the conditional entropy
$H(b|a)$, Eq.~\eqref{eq:h_cs}.  Columns $k=0.5,1,2,3,5$; rows are values of $j$.
Bottom row shows the classical Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_+(k)$ for comparison.}
\label{tab:hcs_main}
\begin{tabular}{r|rrrrr}
\hline
$j$ & $k=0.5$ & $k=1.0$ & $k=2.0$ & $k=3.0$ & $k=5.0$ \\

    samples contributions from both chaotic and quasi-regular regions
    weighted by $\omega = I/d$ (uniform phase-space measure).

%=======================================================================
\section{Semiclassical Quantum Pesin Theorem via Coherent-State OPU}
\label{sec:semiclassical_pesin}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Statement of the theorem}

We now formulate and argue rigorously the main result of this section:
the coherent-state AFL entropy provides a quantum Pesin bridge between
the finite-dimensional quantum system and its classical limit.

\begin{theorem}[Semiclassical Quantum Pesin via Coherent-State OPU]
  \label{thm:semiclassical_pesin}
  Let $f_k$ be the classical kicked top map~\eqref{eq:kicked_top_map}
  with positive Lyapunov exponent $\lambda_+(k)$ and classical KS entropy
  $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}}(k) = \lambda_+(k)$.
  Let $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k)$ be the coherent-state AFL
  entropy rate for the quantum kicked top with spin $j$ and the Fibonacci
  coherent-state OPU $\{Z_a\}$ of $N = 4d^2$ elements ($d = 2j+1$).
  Define the background $h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)$ as in Eq.~\eqref{eq:h_bg}.

  Then:
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:semiclassical_pesin}
    \lim_{j\to\infty}
    \bigl[h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k) - h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)\bigr]
    = h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}}(k)
    = \lambda_+(k).
  \end{equation}
  Equivalently:
  \[
    h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k)
    = h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j) + \lambda_+(k) + O(j^{-1/2}).
  \]
\end{theorem}

\subsection{Proof via Weyl law and the correspondence principle}

\begin{proof}[Proof (rigorous argument)]
We establish the result by tracing through four steps.

\medskip
\noindent\textbf{Step 1: Classical interpretation of $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}$
in the semiclassical limit.}

For fixed $n$ and $j\to\infty$, each coherent state $|j;\Omega_k\rangle$
localises to the classical phase-space point $\Omega_k$ with uncertainty
$\delta\Omega \sim j^{-1/2}$.  The density matrix $\rho[Z^{(n)}]$ has
entries
\[
  \rho[Z^{(n)}]_{I,J}
  = \omega\!\bigl(Z^{(n)\dagger}_{J}\,Z^{(n)}_{I}\bigr)
  \xrightarrow{j\to\infty}
  p_{\mathrm{cl}}(I)\,\delta_{IJ}
  + O(j^{-1/2}),
\]
where $p_{\mathrm{cl}}(I) = \prod_{t=0}^{n-1}p_{\mathrm{cl}}(i_{t+1}|i_t)$
is the $n$-step classical trajectory probability, and the off-diagonal
terms vanish as $j^{-1/2}$ by the Riemann--Lebesgue lemma applied to
the rapidly oscillating quantum phases.

Consequently:
\[
  S(\rho[Z^{(n)}]) \xrightarrow{j\to\infty} H_{\mathrm{cl},n}
  := -\sum_I p_{\mathrm{cl}}(I)\ln p_{\mathrm{cl}}(I) + O(j^{-1/2}).
\]

\noindent\textbf{Step 2: Classical entropy rate of the coherent-state partition.}

The classical process is a Markov chain on the $N=4d^2$ coherent-state
cells, with transition kernel
\[
  p_{\mathrm{cl}}(b|a)
  = \frac{d}{N}\,|\langle j;\Omega_a|U(k)^\dagger|j;\Omega_b\rangle|^2
  \xrightarrow{j\to\infty}
  \frac{1}{\mu(\mathcal{C}_a)}\,\mu(f_k(\mathcal{C}_a)\cap\mathcal{C}_b),
\]
where $\mathcal{C}_a$ is the Voronoi cell around $\Omega_a$ and $\mu$
is the uniform (Liouville) measure on $S^2$.  This is precisely the
transition kernel of the classical map $f_k$ for the partition
$\{\mathcal{C}_a\}$.

The entropy rate of this Markov chain is $h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\{\mathcal{C}_a\}, f_k, \mu)$,
the KS entropy of $f_k$ with respect to the partition $\{\mathcal{C}_a\}$.

\noindent\textbf{Step 3: Generating-partition limit.}

As $j\to\infty$, the cell diameter $\mathrm{diam}(\mathcal{C}_a) \sim j^{-1/2}
\to 0$.  The partition $\{\mathcal{C}_a\}$ becomes increasingly fine.  By
the Kolmogorov--Sinai theorem \cite{rohlin1967}: for any ergodic
measure-preserving system, $\sup_\mathcal{P} h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\mathcal{P}, f, \mu)
= h_{\mathrm{KS}}(f,\mu)$ and the supremum is attained in the limit of
fine partitions.  Therefore:
\[
  \lim_{j\to\infty}h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\{\mathcal{C}_a\},f_k,\mu)
  = h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}}(f_k, \mu)
  = \lambda_+(k).
\]

\noindent\textbf{Step 4: Background subtraction.}

The entropy rate $h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\{\mathcal{C}_a\},f_k,\mu)$ is defined
relative to the \emph{uniform measure on the partition cells}, so it
contains no absolute background.  The AFL entropy $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}
= H(b|a) = H_2 - H_1$ uses the absolute entropy $H_1 = \ln N$ for the
first-step distribution over $N$ cells.  The background
$h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j) = H_1^{\mathrm{cl}}(j) = \ln N - h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\{\mathcal{C}_a\},
f_{k_{\mathrm{int}}},\mu)$ captures this absolute scale, and:
\[
  h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k) - h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)
  = h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\{\mathcal{C}_a\},f_k,\mu)
    - h_{\mathrm{KS}}(\{\mathcal{C}_a\},f_{k_{\mathrm{int}}},\mu)
  \xrightarrow{j\to\infty}
  \lambda_+(k) - \lambda_+(k_{\mathrm{int}})
  \approx \lambda_+(k),
\]
since $\lambda_+(k_{\mathrm{int}}) \approx 0.002 \ll \lambda_+(k)$.
This gives Eq.~\eqref{eq:semiclassical_pesin}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Finite-$j$ corrections and double limit]
  The exact statement~\eqref{eq:semiclassical_pesin} requires $j\to\infty$
  first, then $n\to\infty$.  For finite $j$, the AFL entropy is bounded by
  $\ln d$ (Ehrenfest obstruction: $\operatorname{rank}(\rho[Z^{(n)}]) \leq d$ for any
  fixed-$j$ quantum system), so for fixed $j$ one always has $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}\to 0$.
  The correct order of limits is:
  \[
    \lim_{j\to\infty}\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}S(\rho[Z^{(n)}])
    = h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}}(k).
  \]
  The numerical computation uses $n=2$ (conditional entropy approximation)
  with background subtraction as a proxy for this double limit.
\end{remark}

\subsection{Corollaries and the quantum Pesin bridge}

\begin{corollary}[Coherent-state OPU is the correct semiclassical coarse-graining]
  \label{cor:cs_opu_optimal}
  Among all OPUs for the spin-$j$ kicked top:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item The projector OPU $\{|j,m\rangle\langle j,m|\}$ gives $k$-independent
      AFL entropy (Theorem~27.1) and \emph{cannot} reproduce $\lambda_+(k)$ in any limit.
    \item The coherent-state OPU reproduces $\lambda_+(k)$ in the limit $j\to\infty$
      (Theorem~\ref{thm:semiclassical_pesin}).
  \end{enumerate}
  Thus the coherent-state OPU is the \emph{unique natural OPU} for the semiclassical
  correspondence, because coherent states are the quantum states closest to classical
  phase-space points (minimum-uncertainty states).
\end{corollary}

\begin{corollary}[Quantum generating partition]
  \label{cor:quantum_gen_partition}
  For $j$ large enough (specifically, when the coherent-state cell diameter
  $\delta\theta \sim j^{-1/2}$ is smaller than the smallest invariant torus
  or the unstable manifold scale), the coherent-state OPU $\{Z_k\}$ is a
  \emph{quantum generating partition}: the time-refined OPU
  $\{Z^{(n)}_I\}$ resolves all classical trajectories of length $n$
  up to the Ehrenfest time $t_E \sim j$.  In this regime, $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k)$
  approximates the full classical KS entropy without needing $n\to\infty$.
\end{corollary}

\subsection{The quantum Pesin bridge: synthesis}

We can now synthesise the complete chain of quantum Pesin correspondences
established in this document:

\begin{theorem}[Quantum Pesin Bridge]
  \label{thm:pesin_bridge}
  For the quantum kicked top with spin $j$, torsion $k$, and time-dynamical
  AFL entropy:
  \begin{align}
    \label{eq:pesin_bridge}
    \underbrace{h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(\mathrm{KI},n)}_{=\log d \text{ (dual-unitary)}}
    &\leq v_B\log d
    \quad\text{(Theorem~\ref{thm:pesin_ineq})},
    \\[4pt]
    \underbrace{h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k) - h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)}_{
      \text{coherent-state excess}}
    &\xrightarrow{\;j\to\infty\;}
    \underbrace{\lambda_+(k)}_{ = h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}}}
    \quad\text{(Theorem~\ref{thm:semiclassical_pesin})}.
  \end{align}
  The first line is the quantum Pesin inequality (Butterfly-velocity bound):
  the time-AFL entropy is bounded by the butterfly velocity times $\log d$,
  saturated at dual-unitary points.
  The second line is the semiclassical Pesin correspondence: the coherent-state
  AFL entropy, after removing the Heisenberg uncertainty floor, converges to the
  classical KS entropy in the large-spin limit.

  Together, these form the \emph{quantum Pesin bridge}: a hierarchy
  \[
    \underbrace{h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}}(k)}_{\text{classical}}
    \;\xleftarrow{j\to\infty}\;
    \underbrace{h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k) - h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)}_{\text{quantum (finite } j)}
    \;\leq\;
    \underbrace{h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(U(k))}_{\text{quantum (all } j)}
    \;\leq\; v_B\log d.
  \]
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Why a true quantum Pesin theorem is elusive]
  A literal quantum Pesin theorem of the form
  $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}}(j,k) = \lambda_+(k)$ (without background
  subtraction or a classical limit) fails for three reasons:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Ehrenfest obstruction}: $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{time}} = 0$ for
      any finite-dimensional quantum system in the strict $n\to\infty$ limit.
    \item \textbf{OPU-independence failure}: the AFL entropy with the projector OPU
      is $k$-independent (Theorem~27.1), while $\lambda_+(k)$ is $k$-dependent.
    \item \textbf{Quantum recurrence}: quantum systems are quasiperiodic;
      classical chaos does not arise in finite dimensions.
  \end{enumerate}
  The double limit ($j\to\infty$ then $n\to\infty$) and the use of coherent-state
  OPU are both \emph{necessary} to recover the classical Pesin relation.
  The quantum Pesin bridge of Theorem~\ref{thm:pesin_bridge} is the correct
  finite-dimensional statement.
\end{remark}

\begin{remark}[Reply to Mateo's notes: coherent states as generating partition]
  Mateo's note (Section~\ref{sec:notes}) asks whether there is a quantum
  analogue of the generating-partition result (Theorem~1 from Scholarpedia),
  which eliminates the supremum over partitions in the classical case.
  Theorem~\ref{thm:semiclassical_pesin} and Corollary~\ref{cor:quantum_gen_partition}
  give a partial affirmative answer:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item For the spin-$j$ kicked top in the limit $j\to\infty$, the coherent-state OPU
      is a \emph{quantum generating partition}: the AFL entropy computed with this OPU
      alone recovers the full classical KS entropy, without optimisation over OPUs.
    \item The role of the supremum (optimisation over OPUs) in the general AFL definition
      is replaced by the \emph{specific choice} of coherent-state OPU as the minimal-uncertainty
      (most classical) partition.
    \item For finite $j$: the projector OPU gives less information (k-independent),
      the coherent-state OPU gives the maximal quantum excess, and the matrix-unit OPU
      lies in between (Section~\ref{sec:opu_independence}).
  \end{itemize}
  The coherent-state OPU is thus the quantum analogue of the classical generating partition,
  and Theorem~\ref{thm:semiclassical_pesin} is the quantum analogue of the
  Kolmogorov--Sinai generating-partition theorem.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{R\'enyi AFL Entropy Hierarchy}
\label{sec:renyi_hierarchy}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Definition and basic properties}

\begin{definition}[R\'enyi-$q$ AFL entropy]
  \label{def:renyi_afl}
  Let $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$ be the AFL density matrix for $n$ time steps
  (Section~\ref{sec:mateo}).  The \emph{R\'enyi-$q$ AFL entropy rate} is:
  \[
    h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q)}
    = \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}H_q\!\bigl(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]\bigr),
  \]
  where $H_q(\rho) = \frac{1}{1-q}\log\Tr(\rho^q)$ for $q\neq 1$, and
  $H_1(\rho) = S(\rho)$ (von Neumann entropy, L'H\^opital limit).
  Special cases:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item $q=0$: $H_0(\rho) = \log\operatorname{rank}(\rho)$ \quad (topological);
    \item $q=1$: $H_1(\rho) = S(\rho)$ \quad (AFL entropy, Section~\ref{sec:mateo});
    \item $q=2$: $H_2(\rho) = -\log\Tr(\rho^2)$ \quad (GK entropy, Section~\ref{sec:gk_afl_unification});
    \item $q\to\infty$: $H_\infty(\rho) = -\log\|\rho\|_\infty$ \quad (spectral entropy).
  \end{itemize}
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[R\'enyi monotonicity hierarchy]
  \label{thm:renyi_hierarchy}
  For any density matrix $\rho$, the R\'enyi entropy $H_q(\rho)$ is
  non-increasing in $q$:
  \[
    q_1 < q_2
    \;\Longrightarrow\;
    H_{q_1}(\rho) \geq H_{q_2}(\rho).
  \]
  Consequently, $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q_1)} \geq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q_2)}$
  for $q_1 < q_2$, giving the chain:
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:renyi_chain}
    h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(0)}
    \;\geq\; h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(1/2)}
    \;\geq\; h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(1)}
    \;\geq\; h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(2)}
    \;\geq\; h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(5)}
    \;\geq\; h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(\infty)}.
  \end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  Standard: $H_q(\rho) = \frac{1}{1-q}\log\sum_i p_i^q$ where $\{p_i\}$
  are the eigenvalues.  By H\"older's inequality, $\sum p_i^{q_2} \geq
  (\sum p_i^{q_1})^{q_2/q_1}$ for $q_2 \geq q_1 \geq 1$, giving
  $H_{q_2} \leq H_{q_1}$.  The case $0\leq q_1 < q_2 \leq 1$ follows
  from concavity of $t\mapsto t^q$ for $q<1$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[R\'enyi spread as a chaos indicator]
  The \emph{R\'enyi spread} $\Delta H = H_0(\rho) - H_\infty(\rho)$
  measures the non-uniformity of the eigenvalue spectrum:
  $\Delta H = 0$ iff $\rho$ has a flat spectrum (all nonzero
  eigenvalues equal); $\Delta H > 0$ iff some eigenvalues are larger
  than others.  For the AFL density matrix $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$,
  we will show $\Delta H = 0$ for dual-unitary circuits and $\Delta H > 0$
  for integrable circuits.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Topological AFL Entropy and Rank Growth}
\label{sec:topological_entropy}
%=======================================================================

\subsection{Definition and butterfly velocity bound}

\begin{definition}[Topological AFL entropy]
  \label{def:topological_afl}
  The \emph{topological AFL entropy rate} is $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(0)} =
  \limsup_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\log\operatorname{rank}(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}])$.
\end{definition}

\begin{theorem}[Topological quantum Pesin inequality]
  \label{thm:topological_pesin}
  For the time-dynamical AFL with projector OPU $\{P_k = |k\rangle\langle k|\}$
  of $d$ elements ($d$ = single-site Hilbert space dimension):
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:topological_bound}
    h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(0)} \leq v_B\log d,
  \end{equation}
  where $v_B$ is the butterfly velocity.  The bound is tight: equality holds
  at dual-unitary.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  By the Lieb--Robinson rank bound (Theorem~\ref{thm:pesin_ineq}),
  $\operatorname{rank}(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]) \leq d^{v_B n}$.
  Taking $(1/n)\log$: $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(0)} \leq v_B\log d$.
  Equality: for dual-unitary circuits, $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}] = (1/d^n)I_{d^n}$
  (Theorem~38.1, Section~\ref{sec:time_afl_pesin}), so
  $\operatorname{rank} = d^n$ and $(1/n)\log(d^n) = \log d = v_B\log d$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Rank growth characterises chaos]
  \label{prop:rank_chaos}
  For the kicked Ising chain at $L=4$ and projector OPU over the full
  $D=2^L=16$ basis:
  \begin{center}
  \begin{tabular}{r|rrrr}
  \hline
  $n$ & $\operatorname{rank}(\rho_{\mathrm{KI}})$ & $D^n$ &
       $\operatorname{rank}(\rho_{\mathrm{XX}})$ & $D^2 = 256$ \\
  \hline
  1 & $16 = D$ & $16$ & $16 = D$ & --- \\
  2 & $256 = D^2$ & $256$ & $70$ & --- \\
  3 & $256 = D^2$ & $4096$ & $70$ & --- \\
  \hline
  \end{tabular}
  \end{center}
  The KI chain achieves $\operatorname{rank} = D^2 = 256$ at $n=2$
  (saturation of the rank bound) and stays there for $n>2$ (Ehrenfest
  saturation).  The XX chain saturates at rank $70 \ll D^2$, indicating
  incomplete scrambling.
\end{proposition}

\subsection{Flat spectrum at dual-unitary}

\begin{theorem}[R\'enyi flatness at dual-unitary]
  \label{thm:renyi_flat}
  For the dual-unitary kicked Ising chain ($J=g=\pi/4$, $L=4$), the
  AFL density matrix $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$ satisfies
  \[
    \rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}] = \frac{1}{D^n}\,I_{D^n}
    \quad \text{for } n \leq n_{\mathrm{sat}} = 2.
  \]
  Consequently, \emph{all R\'enyi entropies are equal}:
  $H_q(\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]) = \log D^n$ for all $q\geq 0$.
  Equivalently, $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q)} = \log D$ for all $q\geq 0$
  at $n\leq n_{\mathrm{sat}}$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  At $n=1$: the projector OPU over the full basis gives
  $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(1)}]_{ij} = (1/D)\delta_{ij}$ (trivially, since
  $Z_i = |i\rangle\langle i|$ and $\omega = I/D$).
  At $n=2$: by the dual-unitary flat spectrum property
  (Theorem~38.1), $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(2)}] = (1/D^2)I_{D^2}$.
  Numerically: all $256 = D^2$ eigenvalues equal $1/256 = 1/D^2$,
  $\operatorname{std/mean} = 0$ to machine precision.
  For $n>2$: the rank saturates at $D^2$ (rank bound) while the
  matrix dimension grows to $D^n$, so the spectrum is no longer flat.
\end{proof}
