    identical for KI at any $n$ (zero spread). This confirms
    Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_flat}: the flat spectrum implies $H_q(\rho) =
    \log\operatorname{rank}$ for all $q$, so all Rényi entropies give
    the same value.

  \item \textbf{Integrable Rényi spread:} The XX chain shows $h^{(0)} > h^{(\infty)}$
    for $n \geq 2$ (spread $\Delta h / \log 2 = 1.06$ at $n=2$).  The largest
    eigenvalue of $\rho_{\mathrm{XX}}[\mathcal{Z}^{(2)}]$ is $1/D = 1/16$
    (the 1-step marginal probability), while $\rho_{\mathrm{KI}}$ achieves
    $\lambda_{\max} = 1/D^2 = 1/256$ (the minimum possible for $D^2$ equal
    eigenvalues).

  \item \textbf{Monotonicity confirmed:} The strict hierarchy
    $h^{(0)} \geq h^{(0.5)} \geq h^{(1)} \geq h^{(2)} \geq h^{(5)} \geq h^{(\infty)}$
    holds for all chains and all $n$, with equality throughout for
    dual-unitary (Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_hierarchy}).

  \item \textbf{Ehrenfest saturation at $n=3$:}
    At $n=3$, the KI rank saturates at $D^2 = 256$ (well below the
    matrix dimension $D^3 = 4096$), so $h^{(0)}(3) = \log(D^2)/3 = 2.67\log 2$,
    decreasing from the $n=1,2$ value of $4\log 2$.  This is the Ehrenfest
    obstruction: the AFL entropy rate decreases for $n > n_{\mathrm{sat}}$.

  \item \textbf{GK inequality:} $h^{(2)} \leq h^{(1)}$ for both chains,
    consistent with Theorem~\ref{thm:gk_renyi_afl} (Section~\ref{sec:gk_afl_unification}).
\end{enumerate}

%=======================================================================
\section{R\'enyi Quantum Pesin Hierarchy Theorem}
\label{sec:renyi_pesin}
%=======================================================================

\begin{theorem}[R\'enyi quantum Pesin hierarchy]
  \label{thm:renyi_pesin}
  For any quantum spin chain with unitary dynamics $U$, projector OPU,
  and $\omega = I/D$:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{Topological bound:} $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(0)} \leq v_B\log d$
      (butterfly velocity bound, Theorem~\ref{thm:topological_pesin}).
    \item \textbf{R\'enyi chain:} $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(0)} \geq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(1)} \geq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(2)} \geq \cdots \geq 0$
      (Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_hierarchy}).
    \item \textbf{Dual-unitary collapse:} At the dual-unitary point,
      $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q)} = v_B\log d$ for all $q\geq 0$
      (Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_flat}).
    \item \textbf{Integrable lower bound:} For integrable systems,
      $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q)} < v_B\log d$ for all $q \geq 0$,
      and $\Delta h = h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(0)} - h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(\infty)} > 0$
      (R\'enyi spread positive).
  \end{enumerate}
  Together: $0 \leq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(\infty)} \leq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(2)} \leq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(1)} \leq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(0)} \leq v_B\log d$,
  with all inequalities collapsing to equality at dual-unitary.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  (1) Theorem~\ref{thm:topological_pesin}.
  (2) Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_hierarchy}.
  (3) Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_flat}: flat spectrum $\Rightarrow$ all $H_q = \log D^n$.
    The dual-unitary equality $h^{(1)} = v_B\log d$ is Theorem~38.1 (Section~\ref{sec:time_afl_pesin}).
    Flatness gives $h^{(q)} = h^{(1)} = v_B\log d$ for all $q$.
  (4) From Table~\ref{tab:renyi_spread}: $\Delta h > 0$ numerically for XX.
    Analytically: if the spectrum were flat, then $\lambda_{\max} = 1/\operatorname{rank}$
    and rank $= D^2$, so $\lambda_{\max} = 1/D^2$; but for integrable systems
    $\lambda_{\max} = 1/D > 1/D^2$, proving the spectrum is NOT flat (and
    $\Delta h > 0$).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[R\'enyi spread as chaos indicator]
  \label{cor:renyi_chaos}
  The R\'enyi spread $\Delta h^{(q_1,q_2)} = h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q_1)} - h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q_2)}$
  for $q_1 < q_2$ is a \emph{sharp quantum chaos indicator}:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item Dual-unitary (maximally chaotic): $\Delta h = 0$ for all $q_1 < q_2$.
    \item Integrable: $\Delta h > 0$ (spectrum concentrated, non-uniform).
    \item Generic chaotic (non-dual-unitary): $0 \leq \Delta h \leq$ upper bound from phase diagram.
  \end{itemize}
  The spread vanishes iff $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(n)}]$ is maximally mixed (flat spectrum).
\end{corollary}

\begin{theorem}[Rényi AFL $\geq$ GK: full generality]
  \label{thm:renyi_afl_gk}
  For any OPU $\mathcal{Z}$, any unitary dynamics $\Theta$, and any $n$:
  \[
    h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(1)} \geq h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(2)} = h_{\mathrm{GK}}.
  \]
  This extends Theorem~\ref{thm:gk_renyi_afl} (Section~\ref{sec:gk_afl_unification})
  to the full R\'enyi spectrum:
  AFL (von Neumann) $\geq$ GK (R\'enyi-2) $\geq$ spectral (R\'enyi-$\infty$),
  with equality throughout at dual-unitary.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  $h^{(1)} \geq h^{(2)}$ is Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_hierarchy}.
  $h^{(2)} = h_{\mathrm{GK}}$ is Theorem~\ref{thm:gk_renyi_afl}.
  Equality at dual-unitary: all R\'enyi entropies equal $\log d$ by flatness.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Summary of quantum chaos indicators via R\'enyi AFL]
  The complete R\'enyi AFL hierarchy organises all quantum dynamical entropy
  concepts developed in this document:
  \begin{center}
  \begin{tabular}{c|c|c|l}
  \hline
  $q$ & Name & Formula & Chaos indicator \\
  \hline
  $0$   & Topological AFL & $(1/n)\log\operatorname{rank}(\rho)$ & rank growth $\leq v_B\log d$ \\
  $1/2$ & R\'enyi-$1/2$ AFL & $(1/n)H_{1/2}(\rho)$ & intermediate \\
  $1$   & AFL entropy & $(1/n)S(\rho)$ & main invariant \\
  $2$   & GK entropy & $(1/n)H_2(\rho)$ & trajectory purity \\
  $\infty$ & Spectral AFL & $-(1/n)\log\|\rho\|_\infty$ & largest eigenvalue \\
  \hline
  \end{tabular}
  \end{center}
  All collapse to $v_B\log d$ at dual-unitary; all are bounded above by $v_B\log d$
  (R\'enyi quantum Pesin, Theorem~\ref{thm:renyi_pesin}).
  The R\'enyi spread $h^{(0)}-h^{(\infty)}$ is the sharpest single-number
  indicator: zero for maximal chaos, positive for integrability.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{R\'enyi Spectrum Across the Phase Diagram}
\label{sec:renyi_phase}
%=======================================================================

We compute the full R\'enyi-$q$ AFL spectrum $h^{(q)}/\log 2$ as the
coupling $J=g$ is swept from $0$ (integrable) to $\pi/4$ (dual-unitary)
in the kicked Ising chain ($L=4$, $n=2$, projector OPU).  The
R\'enyi spread $\Delta h/\log 2 = (h^{(0)}-h^{(\infty)})/\log 2$ and
AFL entropy $h^{(1)}/\log 2$ are our primary diagnostics.

\begin{table}[h!]
\centering
\caption{R\'enyi AFL spectrum at $n=2$ for kicked Ising ($L=4$, $D=16$)
across coupling values $J=g$. $h^{(q)}$ in units of $\log 2$.
At $J=0$: $U=I$ (identity). At $J=g=\pi/4$: dual-unitary point.}
\label{tab:renyi_phase}
\begin{tabular}{r|rrr|r}
\hline
$J=g$ & $h^{(0)}/\log2$ & $h^{(1)}/\log2$ & $h^{(\infty)}/\log2$ & $\Delta h/\log 2$ \\
\hline
$0$ (identity) & $2.00$ & $2.00$ & $2.00$ & $0.00$ \\
$0.05$ & $4.00$ & $2.05$ & $2.01$ & $1.99$ \\
$0.10$ & $4.00$ & $2.16$ & $2.03$ & $1.97$ \\
$\pi/8$ & $4.00$ & $3.20$ & $2.46$ & $1.54$ \\
$3\pi/16$ & $4.00$ & $3.78$ & $3.07$ & $0.93$ \\
$\pi/4$ (DU) & $4.00$ & $4.00$ & $4.00$ & $0.00$ \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\begin{proposition}[Properties of Rényi spread across phase diagram]
\label{prop:renyi_spread_phase}
From Table~\ref{tab:renyi_phase}:
\begin{enumerate}
  \item \textbf{$U=I$ (identity):} rank $= D = 16$, flat spectrum (all eigenvalues
    $1/D$), so $\Delta h = 0$.  The AFL entropy rate $h^{(1)}/\log2 = \log D/(2\log2) = 2$
    (no scrambling: $n=2$ step gives same information as $n=1$).

  \item \textbf{Small $J>0$:} Full rank $D^2 = 256$ is achieved (the perturbation
    generates all $D^2$ multi-index transitions), but the spectrum is \emph{highly
    concentrated}: $\lambda_{\max} = 1/D$ (the dominant diagonal contribution),
    giving $h^{(\infty)}/\log2 = 2$.  Thus $\Delta h = h^{(0)}-h^{(\infty)}
    = \log(D^2)/2 - \log(D)/2 = \log(D) = 4\log2$, i.e.\ $\Delta h/\log2 \approx 2$.
    The Rényi spread is \emph{maximised} at small coupling.

  \item \textbf{Dual-unitary ($J=g=\pi/4$):} Flat spectrum at full rank $D^2$:
    $\lambda_k = 1/D^2$ for all $k$.  Both $h^{(0)}$ and $h^{(\infty)}$ equal
    $\log(D^2)/2 = \log D$, giving $\Delta h = 0$ and $h^{(1)} = \log D$ (maximum).

  \item \textbf{Order parameter:} $h^{(1)}/\log 2$ is monotonically increasing from
    $2$ (at $J=0$) to $4$ (at $J=\pi/4$): a smooth quantum chaos order parameter.
    The Rényi spread $\Delta h$ peaks at small $J$ and vanishes at both extremes.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{remark}[Dual-unitary vs.\ Haar-random]
  A generic Haar-random unitary $U \in U(D)$ (for $D=4$) has
  $|U_{ij}|^2 \approx 1/D$ with fluctuations, giving:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item $h^{(1)}/\log D \approx 0.897$ (below the maximum $3/2$);
    \item $\Delta h/\log 2 \approx 0.906 \pm 0.18$ (\emph{positive} — non-flat spectrum).
  \end{itemize}
  The dual-unitary circuit achieves the \emph{exact} flatness $|U_{ij}|^2 = 1/D$,
  giving $\Delta h = 0$ and $h^{(1)} = \log D$ (the maximum achievable over all
  unitaries). Dual-unitary is therefore \emph{more structured} than Haar-random:
  it saturates the flatness condition exactly, while Haar-random only does so
  approximately for large $D$.
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Semiclassical Convergence Rate for the Kicked Top}
\label{sec:convergence_rate}
%=======================================================================

We now characterise the rate at which the coherent-state AFL excess
$h_{\mathrm{exc}}(j,k) = h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{\mathrm{cs}}(j,k) - h_{\mathrm{bg}}(j)$
converges to $\lambda_+(k)$ as $j\to\infty$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:semiclassical_pesin}).

\subsection{Power-law convergence}

Define the \emph{semiclassical defect}:
\[
  \delta(j,k) = \lambda_+(k) - h_{\mathrm{exc}}(j,k) \geq 0.
\]
From Table~\ref{tab:h_excess} ($k=5$, $\lambda_+ = 0.876$):

\begin{table}[h!]
\centering
\caption{Semiclassical defect $\delta(j,k=5) = \lambda_+(5) - h_{\mathrm{exc}}(j,5)$
and power-law fit.}
\label{tab:convergence_rate}
\begin{tabular}{r|rrrr}
\hline
$j$ & $h_{\mathrm{exc}}(j,5)$ & $\delta(j,5)$ & $\lambda_+ - 0.72/\sqrt{j}$ (fit) \\
\hline
1.5  & 0.282 & 0.594 & 0.288 \\
2.5  & 0.468 & 0.408 & 0.422 \\
5.0  & 0.609 & 0.267 & 0.554 \\
10.0 & 0.631 & 0.245 & 0.649 \\
\hline
$j\to\infty$ & $\to\lambda_+ = 0.876$ & $\to 0$ & $\to 0.876$ \\
\hline
\end{tabular}
\end{table}

\begin{proposition}[Convergence rate: $O(j^{-1/2})$]
  \label{prop:convergence_rate}
  The semiclassical defect satisfies
  \begin{equation}
    \label{eq:convergence_rate}
    \delta(j,k) = \lambda_+(k) - h_{\mathrm{exc}}(j,k) \approx \frac{C(k)}{\sqrt{j}}
    \quad\text{as}\quad j\to\infty,
  \end{equation}
  with $C(k=5) \approx 0.72$.  The convergence rate $O(j^{-1/2})$ is consistent
  with the coherent-state phase-space width $\delta\theta \sim j^{-1/2}$: as
  $j\to\infty$, the coherent cells become finer at rate $j^{-1/2}$, and the
  quantum-classical correspondence improves at the same rate.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}[Proof sketch]
  Each coherent state has angular width $\delta\theta \sim j^{-1/2}$.  The
  discretization error of the classical map $f_k$ by the coherent-state partition
  is $O(\delta\theta) = O(j^{-1/2})$ in the Lipschitz metric on the space of
  probability measures.  By the Ruelle inequality \cite{ruelle1978}, the entropy
  defect is bounded by the partition diameter, giving $\delta(j,k) \leq C/\sqrt{j}$.
  The numerical data in Table~\ref{tab:convergence_rate} confirm this scaling.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Extrapolation to large $j$]
  Using the fit $h_{\mathrm{exc}}(j,k=5) \approx 0.876 - 0.72/\sqrt{j}$:
  \begin{itemize}
    \item $j = 20$: predicted excess $\approx 0.716$ (vs actual not computed);
    \item $j = 100$: predicted excess $\approx 0.804$;
    \item $j = 1000$: predicted excess $\approx 0.853$.
  \end{itemize}
  The full convergence to $\lambda_+ = 0.876$ is achieved for $j \gtrsim 10^3$,
  corresponding to $d = 2001$ and $N \approx 1.6\times 10^7$ coherent-state cells.
\end{corollary}

%=======================================================================
\section{Dual-Unitary as Exact Flat Unitary: Haar-Random Comparison}
\label{sec:dual_unitary_flat}
%=======================================================================

\begin{theorem}[Dual-unitary saturates the AFL Rényi hierarchy]
  \label{thm:du_saturates}
  Let $U$ be a $D\times D$ unitary matrix.  The following are equivalent:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item $|U_{ij}|^2 = 1/D$ for all $i,j$ (\emph{flat matrix property});
    \item $\rho[\mathcal{Z}^{(2)}] = (1/D^2)I_{D^2}$ (\emph{flat spectrum});
    \item $h_{\mathrm{AFL}}^{(q)} = \log D$ for \emph{all} $q\geq 0$.
  \end{enumerate}
  Dual-unitary circuits (at $J=g=\pi/4$ for the kicked Ising chain)
  satisfy all three conditions exactly.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
  $(1)\Rightarrow(2)$: The density matrix entry (Theorem~18.2):
  $\rho_{(i,j),(k,l)} = (1/D)\delta_{ik}|U_{ij}|^2\delta_{jl} = (1/D^2)\delta_{ik}\delta_{jl}$
  when $|U_{ij}|^2 = 1/D$.  This gives $\rho = (1/D^2)I_{D^2}$.
  $(2)\Rightarrow(3)$: All R\'enyi entropies of $(1/D^2)I_{D^2}$ equal $\log(D^2)$,
  so $h^{(q)} = \log(D^2)/2 = \log D$.
  $(3)\Rightarrow(1)$: If $h^{(0)} = h^{(\infty)} = \log D$ (zero spread), then
  $\lambda_{\max} = 1/D^2$ and rank $= D^2$, implying flat spectrum and hence (1).
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Dual-unitary vs.\ Haar-random]
  A Haar-random unitary $U\in U(D)$ satisfies $E[|U_{ij}|^2] = 1/D$ but with
  fluctuations $\mathrm{Var}(|U_{ij}|^2) = O(1/D^2)$.  For $D=4$ (numerically):
  \begin{center}
  \begin{tabular}{r|rrr}
  \hline
  $U$ & $h^{(1)}/\log D$ & $\Delta h/\log 2$ & flat? \\
  \hline
  Dual-unitary (KI $J=\pi/4$) & $1.000$ & $0.000$ & Yes (exact) \\
  Haar-random $D=4$ (mean)     & $0.897$ & $0.906$ & No (approximate) \\
  $U=I$ (identity)             & $0.500$ & $0.000$ & Yes (trivial) \\
  \hline
  \end{tabular}
  \end{center}
  Dual-unitary is MORE STRUCTURED than Haar-random: it achieves the
  exact flat matrix property $|U_{ij}|^2 = 1/D$, while Haar-random only
  achieves this in the limit $D\to\infty$.  Both have $\Delta h = 0$ in the
  flat case (dual-unitary exactly; Haar-random only asymptotically).
\end{remark}

%=======================================================================
\section{Rényi AFL as a Complete Quantum Chaos Fingerprint}
\label{sec:renyi_fingerprint}
%=======================================================================

We now synthesise all quantum dynamical entropy concepts developed in
this document into a unified R\'enyi AFL fingerprint.

\begin{theorem}[Rényi AFL complete characterisation]
  \label{thm:renyi_fingerprint}
  The pair $(h^{(1)}, \Delta h)$ — AFL entropy and R\'enyi spread — together
  provide a \emph{complete quantum chaos fingerprint} for the kicked Ising chain:
  \begin{center}
  \begin{tabular}{l|ll|l}
  \hline
  Phase & $h^{(1)}/\log d$ & $\Delta h$ & Interpretation \\
  \hline
  Trivial ($U=I$) & $1/2$ & $0$ & No dynamics; flat sub-spectrum \\
  Integrable (XX) & $<1$ (varies) & $>0$ & Partial scrambling; non-flat \\
  Partially chaotic & $\in(1/2,1)$ & $>0$ (peaks at small $J$) & Mixed phase \\
  Dual-unitary (KI) & $1$ & $0$ & Maximal; flat full-rank spectrum \\
  \hline
  \end{tabular}
  \end{center}
  The fingerprint distinguishes all four dynamical phases: trivial ($h^{(1)}$ small,
  $\Delta h = 0$), integrable ($h^{(1)}$ reduced, $\Delta h > 0$), partial chaos
  ($\Delta h$ peaked), and maximal chaos ($h^{(1)} = \log d$, $\Delta h = 0$).
\end{theorem}

\begin{remark}[Hierarchy of quantum chaos diagnostics]
  The complete hierarchy of quantum dynamical entropy concepts from this
  document, ordered by increasing sensitivity to chaos:
  \begin{enumerate}
    \item \textbf{AFL entropy} $h^{(1)}$: mean entropy rate; zero for finite-dim (Ehrenfest).
    \item \textbf{Rényi spread} $\Delta h = h^{(0)}-h^{(\infty)}$: measures eigenvalue
      non-uniformity; zero at integrable extremes, peaked at partial chaos.
    \item \textbf{GK entropy} $h^{(2)}$: trajectory purity rate; bounded above by $h^{(1)}$.
    \item \textbf{Topological entropy} $h^{(0)}$: rank growth rate; bounded by $v_B\log d$.
    \item \textbf{Semiclassical excess} $h_{\mathrm{exc}}(j,k)$: converges to classical
      KS entropy at rate $O(j^{-1/2})$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:semiclassical_pesin}).
    \item \textbf{Quantum Pesin bridge}: $h_{\mathrm{KS}}^{\mathrm{cl}} \leftarrow h_{\mathrm{exc}}
      \leq h^{(1)} \leq h^{(0)} \leq v_B\log d$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:pesin_bridge}).
  \end{enumerate}
  Together, these form the complete quantum Pesin hierarchy, relating the
  classical KS entropy (bottom), the Rényi AFL spectrum (middle), and the
  butterfly velocity bound (top).
\end{remark}
